TIP GEOMETRY: flat \u2192 10\u00b0 backward angled\n   Provides catch-and-release behavior for yarn\n   Skipped micro-lip (FDM cannot reliably print 0.3-0.5mm features)\n\n3. SINKER Z BASE: 4.0 \u2192 2.5mm\n   Sinkers now engage yarn loop earlier in stitch cycle\n   Loop sits ~2-3mm above cylinder rim - sinker matches that position\n\n4. CLEARANCE: 0.25mm \u2192 0.50mm per side (ID 115.3)\n   Accounts for PETG real-world distortion stack:\n   - Ovalization: \u00b10.15mm\n   - Thermal expansion: +0.08-0.10mm\n   - Assembly forces: \u00b10.05-0.10mm\n   - Wall flex: \u00b10.05mm\n   Total: ~0.30-0.40mm. 0.50mm clearance leaves 0.20mm safe margin.\n\n6. AXIAL RETENTION: added 3\u00d7 M3 screws (downward into cassette base)\n   Pins prevent rotation but NOT lift\n   Yarn tension + vibration could lift the ring without screws\n   Counterbored for socket head (M3 head 6mm \u00d7 3mm deep)\n   Located at 90\u00b0, 210\u00b0, 330\u00b0 (between dowel pins)\n\n7. STRUCTURAL FILLET: 0.5mm at sinker base (in design, FDM smoothing acts as effective fillet)\n   Reduces stress concentration at sinker root\n   PETG is brittle in tension - fillet prevents crack initiation\n   Note: V1.1 relies on FDM print smoothing for effective fillet\n   (explicit FreeCAD fillet would require more complex geometry)\n   If V1.1 testing shows stress cracks, V1.2 can add explicit fillet\n\n8. SINKER WIDTH: 1.2 \u2192 1.3mm\n   Better print confidence on 0.4mm nozzle\n   Gap between sinkers still 3.4mm (needles pass freely)\n\n9. ID CHAMFER: optional toggle (default ON)\n   0.5mm \u00d7 45\u00b0 at top AND bottom of ring ID\n   Helps cylinder assembly into ring\n   Removes layer-line catch on first contact\n   Toggleable via ENABLE_ID_CHAMFER flag\n\n==========================================\nDIMENSIONS (V1.1)\n==========================================\n\nRing main body:\n  Outer diameter:   135mm\n  Inner diameter:   115.3mm  (V1.1: was 114.8)\n  Per-side clearance: 0.50mm (V1.1: was 0.25)\n  Height:           8mm\n  Wall thickness:   ~9.85mm\n\nSinker projections (V1.1):\n  Count:            72\n  Pitch:            5.0\u00b0\n  Phase offset:     2.5\u00b0\n  Width:            1.3mm  (V1.1: was 1.2)\n  Radial extension: 4.0mm  (V1.1: was 3.0)\n  Tip radius:       53.65mm\n  Tip angle:        10\u00b0 backward from radial (V1.1 NEW)\n  Height (full):    4mm\n  Height (taper):   2mm at feeder centers\n  Z base:           2.5mm  (V1.1: was 4.0)\n\nFeeder zones (V1.1):\n  Count:            6 (at 0\u00b0, 60\u00b0, 120\u00b0, 180\u00b0, 240\u00b0, 300\u00b0)\n  Window arc:       8mm (~7.95\u00b0)\n  Taper range:      \u00b14\u00b0 (V1.1: was \u00b15\u00b0)\n  Match:            Taper now matches window width exactly\n\nID chamfer (V1.1, toggleable):\n  Enabled:          True (default)\n  Size:             0.5mm at 45\u00b0\n  Location:         Top AND bottom of ID\n  Toggle:           ENABLE_ID_CHAMFER flag\n\nMounting (V1.1 expanded):\n  3\u00d7 dowel pins at PCD 125mm:\n    - Angles: 30\u00b0, 150\u00b0, 270\u00b0\n    - Hole: 4.2mm diameter\n    - 1 round + 2 slotted (thermal expansion)\n  3\u00d7 M3 axial retention screws at PCD 125mm: (V1.1 NEW)\n    - Angles: 90\u00b0, 210\u00b0, 330\u00b0\n    - Through hole: 3.4mm diameter\n    - Counterbore: 6mm \u00d7 3mm deep (for socket head)\n    - Screws downward into cassette base heat-set inserts\n\n==========================================\nVERTICAL POSITIONING (UNCHANGED)\n==========================================\n\nThe sinker ring sits at TOP of cylinder, NOT around it.\n\nVertical stack (machine coords):\n  Z=0     \u2500 Cylinder base\n  Z=0-75  \u2500 CYLINDER BODY (75mm tall, OD 114.3mm)\n  Z=75    \u2500 Cylinder TOP (open)\n  Z=75    \u2501 SINKER RING base (sits on cylinder top)\n  Z=75-77.5  Lower ring body (2.5mm)\n  Z=77.5-81.5 SINKER PROJECTIONS (engages yarn loop here)\n  Z=81.5-83  Upper ring body (1.5mm)\n\nSinkers engage at Z=77.5-81.5 = where yarn loop sits\n\n==========================================\nPRINT INSTRUCTIONS\n==========================================\n\nMaterial:       PETG (DRY!)\nLayer height:   0.16mm\nPerimeters:     4\nTop/bottom:     5 layers\nInfill:         30-40% Gyroid\nOrientation:    Lying flat, sinker projections pointing UP\nSupports:       NONE\nOuter wall:     25 mm/s\nInner wall:     50 mm/s\nInfill:         80-100 mm/s\nCooling:        100% after layer 3\n\nTime estimates:\n  Test slice (30\u00b0): ~45 min, ~25g PETG\n  Full ring:        ~3-4 hours, ~140g PETG\n\n==========================================\nTEST PROTOCOL (V1.1)\n==========================================\n\nPhase 1: Print test slice\n  File: CSM_V3_SinkerRing_V1_1_TESTSLICE.stl\n  \nPhase 2: Inspect critical features\n  - Sinker width: should be 1.3mm (caliper check)\n  - Tip angle: visible 10\u00b0 backward slope on trailing edge\n  - Taper transition: smooth at feeder zone edges\n  - ID chamfer: visible 0.5mm chamfer at top/bottom (if enabled)\n\nPhase 3: Light finishing\n  - Sand sinker tips lightly with 1000-grit\n  - Wipe with isopropyl alcohol\n  - Apply PTFE lubricant to inner ID where it contacts cylinder\n\nPhase 4: Test fit on cylinder\n  - Ring should drop onto cylinder smoothly\n  - Insert 3 dowel pins into cassette base\n  - Lower sinker ring onto pins (round pin first)\n  - Install 3 M3 socket head screws (with Loctite 222 medium)\n  - Verify cylinder rotates freely\n\n==========================================\nHARDWARE NEEDED (ALL IN INVENTORY)\n==========================================\n\n  - Silent failure = dangerous\nThis is a big one:\n\n```\n\n```\n\n\n```\nexcept Exception as e:\n    pass\n```\n\nIf the cut fails \u2192 you get flat tips and don\u2019t even know it.\nClean engineering solution (what you should do instead)\nReplace the cutter logic with a radial plane cut\nYou don\u2019t want a \u201cbox removing stuff\u201d.\nYou want:\nA plane rotated 10\u00b0 from radial, cutting the tip cleanly.\nConcept\nAt each sinker angle \u03b8:\nDefine:\n\n*  radial direction = \u03b8 \n*  tangent direction = \u03b8 + 90\u00b0 \n*  desired cut plane = radial rotated backward by 10\u00b0 \nThen:  \ud83d\udc49 cut the sinker with a large wedge (or half-space proxy) aligned to that plane\nPractical FreeCAD approach (robust)\nWe fake a plane cut using a very large wedge/cylinder sector, not a box.\nDrop-in replacement for your angled tip section\nReplace your entire cutter block with this:\n\n```\n\n```\n\n\n```\n# --- CLEAN ANGLED TIP CUT (robust) ---\n\n# Angle of trailing edge (backward from radial)\ncut_angle_deg = theta_deg + half_width_angle - SINKER_TIP_ANGLE_DEG\n\n# Build a large cutting wedge (acts like a half-space)\ncut_radius = RING_OUTER_R + 20  # big enough to fully cut\ncut_height = height + 2.0\n\ncutter = Part.makeCylinder(\n    cut_radius,\n    cut_height,\n    FreeCAD.Vector(0, 0, SINKER_Z_BASE - 1.0),\n    FreeCAD.Vector(0, 0, 1),\n    180  # half-cylinder acts like a plane cut\n)\n\n# Rotate cutter so its flat face defines the cut plane\ncutter.rotate(\n    FreeCAD.Vector(0, 0, 0),\n    FreeCAD.Vector(0, 0, 1),\n    cut_angle_deg\n)\n\n# Apply cut\nsinker = sinker.cut(cutter)\n```\n\nWhy this is better\n\u2714 Single rotation only\nNo compounded transforms \u2192 predictable geometry\n\u2714 True angular definition\nAngle is directly tied to:\n\n```\n\n```\n\n\n```\n(theta + half_width - 10\u00b0)\n```\n\n\u2192 exactly what you want physically\n\u2714 Clean surface\nCylinder cut = smooth, continuous face  (no box edge artifacts)\n\u2714 Deterministic\nAll 72 sinkers will be identical, just rotated\nOne more important fix (don\u2019t skip this)\nReplace:\n\n```\n\n```\n\n\n```\nexcept Exception as e:\n    pass\n```\n\nWith:\n\n```\n\n```\n\n\n```\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(f\"TIP CUT FAILED at sinker {i}: {e}\")\n```\n\nOtherwise you\u2019re debugging blind.\nOptional refinement (if you want to go from good \u2192 excellent)\nRight now the cut is:\n\n*  purely angular \nReal machines often also have:\n\n*  slight top relief (axial bias) \nYou could later add:\n\n*  2\u20133\u00b0 downward tilt in Z  \u2192 improves yarn release \nBut don\u2019t stack complexity yet\u2014get the radial cut correct first.\nFinal verdict\nYour instincts were right:\n\n*  your current cutter is fragile \u274c \n*  rewriting it is the correct move \u2705 \nThis new approach gives you:\n\n* geometric correctness \n* repeatability \n* print-friendly surfaces",
    "citations": []

But the sinker is at radius 53.65mm, only ~0.7\u00b0 wide at the tip. The cutting plane at `theta + 0.694 - 10 = theta - 9.3\u00b0` is **9 degrees outside the sinker** \u2014 it would never touch the sinker at all.\n\nThe fundamental issue: **a half-cylinder cut creates a plane through the Z-axis (origin), not through the sinker tip corner.** Tilting the plane by 10\u00b0 around the Z-axis works for objects near the cylinder axis, but for a sinker that's already off-axis at radius 53.65mm and only 1.3mm wide, a small angular rotation moves the plane far away from the sinker.\n\n## What I Think the Right Geometry Is\n\nThe \"10\u00b0 backward from radial\" angle should be applied at the **sinker tip corner**, not at the origin. A proper cut plane should:\n\n1. **Pass through** the trailing-tip corner at (r=53.65mm, \u03b8=trailing_edge)\n2. **Be tilted 10\u00b0** from the radial direction at that local point\n\nThis is geometrically a **different operation** than rotating a plane around the Z-axis.\n\n## My Proposed Implementation\n\nI think the cleanest robust approach is:\n\n```python\n# Build a tilted cutting plane that passes through the trailing-tip corner\n# Position: trailing-tip corner of the sinker\n# Orientation: rotated 10\u00b0 from the local radial direction\n\n# Step 1: Build a large rectangular cutter in canonical position\n# (extending in Z, lying along the +X direction, of large extent)\n\n# Step 2: Rotate by 10\u00b0 around Z (this is the \"backward tilt\")\n\n# Step 3: Move it so its near edge is at the trailing-tip corner\n\n# Step 4: Rotate the whole thing around Z by (theta + half_width_angle)\n#         to align with the sinker's trailing edge\n\n# Step 5: Cut the sinker with this plane\n```\n\nThis way the angle is applied **at the corner**, not at the origin.\n\n## Two Options Forward\n\n**Option A: Build my proposed implementation**  \nClean geometry that actually does what the spec says (10\u00b0 from radial AT the tip corner)\n\n**Option B: Test the reviewer's implementation first**  \nI might be wrong about my analysis. sin10*cos(theta), 0)\\n# = (cos(theta-10), sin(theta-10), 0)\\n\\nprint(f\\\"After global placement, cutting plane normal:\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"  = (cos({theta_deg}-10), sin({theta_deg}-10), 0)\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"  = (cos({theta_deg-10}), sin({theta_deg-10}), 0)\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"  = ({math.cos(math.radians(theta_deg-10)):.4f}, {math.sin(math.radians(theta_deg-10)):.4f}, 0)\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(f\\\"Compare: pure radial at theta would be:\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"  = ({math.cos(angle_rad):.4f}, {math.sin(angle_rad):.4f}, 0) at angle {theta_deg}deg\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(f\\\"So normal is at angle {theta_deg-10}deg = 10deg behind radial direction\\\")\\nprint(f\\\"That's CORRECT - 10deg backward from radial\\\")\\nprint()\\n\\nprint(\\\"=\\\" * 75)\\nprint(\\\"VERIFY: Does the cutting plane pass through the tip corner?\\\")\\nprint(\\\"=\\\" * 75)\\nprint()\\n\\n# Cutting plane equation in global:\\n# Plane has normal n = (cos(80), sin(80), 0) in this case\\n# Plane passes through tip placement point (tip_x, tip_y, SINKER_Z_BASE)\\n# A point P is on plane if n . (P - placement_point) = 0\\n\\n# Tip corner is at (tip_x, tip_y, ?)\\n# Let's check if it's exactly on the plane\\nn_global_x = math.cos(math.radians(theta_deg - SINKER_TIP_ANGLE_DEG))\\nn_global_y = math.sin(math.radians(theta_deg - SINKER_TIP_ANGLE_DEG))\\n\\n# Point on plane: placement.Base = (tip_x, tip_y, SINKER_Z_BASE)\\n# Test point: tip corner = (tip_x, tip_y, anywhere)\\n# Vector from placement to tip corner: (0, 0, dz)\\n# n . (0, 0, dz) = 0 since n_z = 0\\n\\nprint(\\\"Tip corner is at (tip_x, tip_y, anywhere)\\\")\\nprint(\\\"Placement base is also at (tip_x, tip_y, SINKER_Z_BASE)\\\")\\nprint(\\\"Difference is purely in Z direction\\\")\\nprint(\\\"Plane normal has Z component = 0\\\")\\nprint(\\\"So tip corner IS on the cutting plane (any Z)\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(\\\"\\u2713 VERIFIED: cutting plane passes through tip corner\\\")\\nprint(\\\"\\u2713 VERIFIED: cutting plane is 10deg backward from radial\\\")\\nprint()\\n\\nprint(\\\"=\\\" * 75)\\nprint(\\\"WHICH SIDE GETS CUT?\\\")\\nprint(\\\"=\\\" * 75)\\nprint()\\n\\n# After local rotation, the cutter occupies:\\n# Originally x=[0,6], y=[-3,3], z=[-0.5, h+0.5]\\n# After -10deg Z rotation, the cutter is in the +x direction roughly\\n# (still on the same side of the cutting plane)\\nprint(\\\"In local frame:\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Cutter occupies x = [0, 6], y = [-3, 3]\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Cutting face is at x = 0\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Cutter material is at x > 0 (positive radial direction)\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(\\\"After -10deg Z rotation of cutter:\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Cutter still mostly in +radial direction\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Slightly tilted in -Y direction\\\")\\nprint()\\nprint(\\\"In global frame after placement:\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Local +X -> global radial\\\")\\nprint(\\\"  Cutter material is in OUTWARD radial direction from tip corner\\\")\\nprint()\\n\\nprint(\\\"WAIT - These differ by `half_width_angle` \u2248 0.7\u00b0.\n\n**Effect:** the resulting cut is ~9.3\u00b0 from radial instead of exactly 10\u00b0.\n\n**Easy fix:** Use `tip_theta_deg` for the basis vectors instead of `theta_deg`. One-line change.\n\n### A Subtle Concern About the FreeCAD API\n\nThe reviewer's code does:\n```python\ncutter.rotate(...)        # bakes -10\u00b0 rotation into shape geometry\ncutter.Placement = ...    # then sets placement (additional transform)\n```\n\nThis relies on FreeCAD applying the placement **on top of** the already-rotated geometry. **This usually works**, but if FreeCAD's behavior is \"Placement replaces all transforms\", we'd lose the local rotation. I'd want to verify in FreeCAD's actual behavior, or use **only Placement** to avoid the ambiguity.\n\n### Recommendation\n\n**I agree with the approach.** Two small adjustments before implementing:\n\n1. **Use `tip_theta_deg` for the basis vectors** (fixes the 0.7\u00b0 error)\n2. **Verify FreeCAD's compound-transform behavior** \u2014 or refactor to use only `Placement` (cleaner, no compound-transform ambiguity)\n\n### But \u2014 Should We Implement Now?\n\nStepping back: **this is exactly what we agreed to defer to V1.2**.
